Stationary log-gamma Polymer in half-space Thanks: Email: jz3524@columbia.edu Thanks: Email: xz3272@columbia.edu
Jiyue Zeng, Xinyi Zhang

Abstract
. We study the half-space log-gamma polymer model with stationary initial conditions. We derive exact formulas for the distribution of the partition function along the diagonal across the entire High density phase and Low density phase. We obtain asymptotics of these distributions under the critical scaling. We also prove the first exponential upper bounds for the upper and lower tail of the scaled free energy for these half-space stationary log-gamma models.



1.
Introduction

Log-gamma polymer model, introduced in
18
, is an exactly solvable model in the KPZ universality class. It can access KPZ equation and zero temperature models such as Last Passage Percolation (LPP), Totally Asymmetric Simple Exclusion Process (TASEP) under certain limits. Exact Laplace transform formulas for the full-space log-gamma free energy were derived using tropical combinatorics and Whittaker function identities
15
, and their asymptotics were later analyzed via nested contour integral techniques introduced in
13
.

Restricting to the half-space geometry introduces boundary interactions
that affects the overall behavior and fluctuations. In
9
, the authors established a distributional identity between half- and full-space log-gamma free energies. Based on the distributional identity
9
and half-space Whittaker process,
2
proved the KPZ exponents for the half-space log-gamma without access to exact formulas. Explicit Fredholm Pfaffian formulas for the point-to-point partition function of half-space log-gamma polymer were later obtained in
17
.

The stationary measures of these models are of particular interests.
For instance, stationary measures have been characterized for the open KPZ equation
14
,
8
, for LPP and log-gamma polymer on a strip
4
, and for the half-space KPZ equation
5
,
6
. Moreover,
16
proves the convergence of half-space log-gamma polymer to the stationary measure along anti-diagonal path. In particular,
5
proved that the half-space log-gamma polymer model admits a two-parameter family of stationary measures, one boundary parameter and one parameter that describes the drift of the initial condition; this family of stationary measures is the main focus of the present work.

The same two-parameter stationary structure appears in half-space LPP models, where under a special choice of parameters it degenerates to Brownian motion.
Fluctuations of half-space LPP with stationary initial condition have been studied in several settings:
11
derived the limiting one-point distribution for exponential LPP with Brownian stationary initial condition;
19
obtained the one-point distribution and critical limits for the two-parameter stationary half-space geometric LPP. It is then natural to further investigate limiting distributions of the half-space log-gamma model with the two-parameter family of stationary initial conditions.

In this work, we derive explicit formulas for
the one-point distribution of the free energy and obtain their critical scaling limits, which should describe the one-point distribution of conjectural half-space KPZ fixed point with stationary initial condition.
We introduce the model and stationary measures explicitly in the following section.

1.1.
Half-space log-gamma polymer with initial conditions

Given the initial condition
(Z(N,1))_{N\in\Z_{\geq 1}}
, we define the partition function
Z(N,M)
of the half-space log-gamma polymer via the following recurrence relation:

(1.1)

\begin{cases}Z(N,M)=\omega_{N,M}(Z(N-1,M)+Z(N,M-1)),&\text{ for }N>M\geq 2,\\
Z(N,N)=\omega_{N,N}Z(N,N-1),&\text{ for }N\geq 2\end{cases}

where the weights
\omega_{N,M}
are all independent and distributed as

(1.2)

\begin{cases}\omega_{N,M}\sim\text{Gamma}^{-1}(2\alpha),&\text{ for }N>M\geq 2,\\
\omega_{N,N}\sim\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\beta+\alpha),&\text{ for }N\geq 2.\\
\end{cases}

for
\alpha>0
and
\beta+\alpha>0.
We call
\log Z(N,M)
the free energy of the polymer.

Definition 1.1
.

We say a process
(\mathcal{T}(x))_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}
is stationary for the half-space log-gamma polymer if the solution to (
1.1
) with the initial condition
Z(1+\cdot,1)/Z(1,1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\mathcal{T}(\cdot)
has the property that the law of
(Z(N+x,N)/Z(N,N))_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}
is the same as the law of
(\mathcal{T}(x))_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}
for all
N\geq 1
.

Definition 1.2
.

For
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and
\beta,t\in\mathbb{R}
with
\alpha>t>0
and
\beta>-t
, we define a process

(1.3)

\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t}(x):=R_{2}(x)+\frac{1}{\omega}\sum_{\ell=1}^{x}R_{1}(\ell)\frac{R_{2}(x)}{R_{2}(\ell-1)}\quad\text{ for }x\in\Z_{\geq 0},

where
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
are independent
\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\alpha-t)
and
\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\alpha+t)
multiplicative random walks with
R_{1}(0)=R_{2}(0)=1,
and
\omega\sim\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\beta+t)
is independent of
R_{1},R_{2}
. When
\beta=-t,
we set
\frac{1}{\omega}=0.
We call
\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t}(\cdot)
the
two-parameter stationary measure
.

It is clear that
\mathcal{T}_{-t,t}
has the law of a multiplicative
\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\alpha+t)
random walk. When
\beta=t
,
\mathcal{T}_{t,t}
is also a multiplicative
\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\alpha+t)
random walk. The proof of this fact can be found in
[
5
, Lemma 2.9]
. We call
\mathcal{T}_{-t,t}
the
product stationary measure
.

We state
[
5
, Theorem 1.8]
in the setting of our parameters.

Theorem 1.3
.

For any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
\beta,t\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\alpha>t>0
and
\beta>\max\{-t,-\alpha\}
, the process
\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t}(\cdot)
in (
1.3
) is stationary for the half-space log-gamma polymer model (
1.1
).

\scriptstyle t=0.5
\beta
-t
\beta=-t
\beta=t
0
HD,
\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t}
MC,
\mathcal{T}_{\beta,0}
LD,
\mathcal{T}_{-t,t}
Q^{\mathrm{MC}}_{\beta,N}
Q_{N}^{Low}
Q_{N,t}^{Low}
Q_{N,\beta>t}^{High}
{Q}_{N,\beta<t}^{High}

Figure 1
.
This picture describes the phase diagram of the half-space log-gamma polymer model given the boundary parameter
\beta
and the parameter
t
that describes the drift. Depending on where
(\beta,t)
lies in the diagram, the process
Z(\cdot,N)/Z(N,N)
converges to one of the three spatial processes,
\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t},

\mathcal{T}_{\beta,0},
or
\mathcal{T}_{-t,t},
which is claimed in Conjecture
1.4
. On the full line
\beta=t
and the half line
\beta=-t
,
\beta\leq 0
,
t\geq 0
, the process converges to the
\text{Gamma}^{-1}

(\alpha+t)
multiplicative random walk.
MC
,
HD
, and
LD
represent Maximal current (green), High density (blue), and Low density (red) phases. We characterize the distribution of
Z(N,N)
in the High density phase under different conditions of
\beta
and
t
. When
\beta>t,
we obtain
Q_{N,\beta>t}^{High}
and when
\beta<t
, we obtain
Q_{N,\beta<t}^{High}
. Along the Low density boundary, we obtain
Q_{N,t}^{Low}
. In particular, we have
{Q}_{N,t}^{Low}
on the line
\beta=t
,
t>0
in High density phase. We obtain these distributions under the condition
t<0.5
. We do not have the distribution for the Maximal current phase, which is drawn as a dashed line.

Phase diagram

We define the digamma function and polygamma functions. Let

(1.4)

\psi(z):=\frac{1}{\Gamma(z)}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}z}\Gamma(z),\qquad\qquad\psi^{(n)}(z)=\frac{\mathrm{d}^{n}}{\mathrm{d}z^{n}}\psi(z).

Conjecture 1.4
.

For given
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
\beta,t\in\mathbb{R}
with
\alpha>t>0
and
\beta>-t
,
\{\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t}\}
constitutes all extremal stationary measures for (
1.1
). For any initial data
Z(x,1)
such that
\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\log Z(x,1)/x=-d\in\mathbb{R}
, we have the following. Let

(1.5)

t=\alpha-\psi^{-1}(d).

•

(High density) For
\beta\geq-t
and
t\geq 0,
the process
\left(Z(x+N,N)/Z(N,N)\right)_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}
converges weakly as
N\rightarrow\infty
to
(\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t}(x))_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}
.

•

(Maximal current) For
\beta\geq 0
and
t\leq 0
, the process
\left(Z(x+N,N)/Z(N,N)\right)_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}

converges weakly as
N\rightarrow\infty
to
(\mathcal{T}_{\beta,0}(x))_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}
.

•

(Low density) For
-t\geq\beta
and
\beta\leq 0,
the process
\left(Z(x+N,N)/Z(N,N)\right)_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}
converges weakly as
N\rightarrow\infty
to
(\mathcal{T}_{-t,t}(x))_{x\in\Z_{\geq 0}}
, i.e., a
\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\alpha+t)
multiplicative random walk.

The parameter
t
describes the drift of the initial condition through the relation (
1.5
). Depending on the
(\beta,t)
values, the system exhibits three different
regimes. We give a heuristic interpretation of each regime:

(1)

High-density regime.

The free energy is dominated by contributions of weights from the first row (the initial condition).

(2)

Low-density regime.

By symmetry with the High density regime, the dominant contribution to the free energy
comes from weights on the diagonal, whereas the influence of
the initial condition becomes negligible.

(3)

Maximal-current regime.

In this regime, weights from the initial condition and the diagonal have comparable
strength, leading to a competition between the two. Thus, the dominant contribution comes from the bulk weights.

1.2.
Main results

We define the diagamma function and polygamma functions. Let

(1.6)

\psi(z):=\frac{1}{\Gamma(z)}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}z}\Gamma(z),\qquad\qquad\psi^{(n)}(z)=\frac{\mathrm{d}^{n}}{\mathrm{d}z^{n}}\psi(z).

Then we define two constants for the following theorems. Let

(1.7)

f=2\psi(\alpha),\quad\sigma=-\psi^{(2)}(\alpha).

1.2.1.
Main results for Low density phase

Definition 1.5
.

Consider the product stationary process
\mathcal{T}_{-t,t}
. We use
\mathcal{Z}^{t}(N,N)
to denote the partition function defined by (
1.1
) and (
1.2
) with
\beta=-t
and set the initial condition
\mathcal{Z}^{t}(1+\cdot,1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\mathcal{T}_{-t,t}.

Theorem 1.6

(Low density phase)
.

Fix any two parameters
t,\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
satisfying
t\in(0,0.5)
and
\alpha>t.
Let
\mathcal{Z}^{t}(N,N)
be defined as in definition
1.5
. Choose
N_{0}\in\Z_{>0}
such that
t>\frac{1}{N_{0}}
. Then for all
N\geq N_{0},
we have

(1.8)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\bigg[2K_{0}\left(2{e^{\left(\log{{\mathcal{Z}}^{t}}(N,N)-\tau\right)/2}}\right)\bigg]=Q_{N,t}^{Low}(\tau)

where
Q_{N,t}^{Low}(\tau)
is defined in Definition
3.1
.

Theorem 1.7

(Low density phase)
.

Fix any
\tilde{t},\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Consider the scaling

(1.9)

t=\frac{\tilde{t}}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}},\quad\tau=-fN+(\sigma N)^{1/3}s.

Let
\mathcal{Z}^{t}(N,N)
be defined as in definition
1.5
. There exists
N_{0}
such that for all
N\geq N_{0},
the identity (
1.8
) holds. Then we have the following asymptotic limit:

(1.10)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(\sigma N)^{-1/3}\mathbbm{E}\bigg[2K_{0}\left(2{e^{\left(\log\mathcal{Z}^{t}(N,N)+Nf-(\sigma N)^{1/3}s\right)/2}}\right)\bigg]=\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}(s)

where
\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}(s)
is defined in Definition
3.2
.

Theorem 1.8

(Low density phase)
.

Fix any
\alpha,\tilde{t}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Consider the scaling

(1.11)

t=\frac{\tilde{t}}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}},\quad\tau=-Nf+(\sigma N)^{1/3}s.

Let
\mathcal{Z}^{t}(N,N)
be defined as in definition
1.5
. Let
\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}
be defined as in Definition
3.2
with a choice of
0<\delta<\tilde{t}
for the contour. Then for any
2\tilde{t}>A>\tilde{t}-\delta
, we have

(1.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}^{t}(N,N)+Nf}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}}\leq s\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(-A+{\rm i}y)e^{-Ax}e^{{\rm i}xy}\left(\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}(x+s)\right)dxdy.

1.2.2.
Main results for High density phase

Definition 1.9
.

Let
X
be an independent
\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\beta+t)
random variable. Let
R_{1},R_{2}
be independent
\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\alpha-t)
and
\text{Gamma}^{-1}(\alpha+t)
random walks with
R_{1}(0)=R_{2}(0)=1.
Let
\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t}
be the two parameter stationary process constructed from
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
and
\omega=X
. We use
\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(N,N)
to denote the partition function defined by (
1.1
) and (
1.2
) with
\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(1,1)=X
and
\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(1+\cdot,1)/\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(1,1)=\mathcal{T}_{\beta,t}(\cdot).

Theorem 1.10

(High density phase with
\beta>t
)
.

Fix any three parameters
t,\beta,\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
satisfying
t\in(0,0.5)
and
\alpha,\beta>t
. Let
\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}
be defined as in definition
1.9
. Choose
N_{0}\in\Z_{>0}
such that
t>\frac{1}{N_{0}}
. Then for all
N\geq N_{0}
, we have

(1.13)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}\mathbbm{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}2K_{0}\left(2\sqrt{e^{-\tau}\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(N-1,N-1)w}\right)w^{t-\beta-1}e^{-w^{-1}}dw\bigg]={Q}_{N,\beta>t}^{High}(\tau)

where
{Q}_{N,\beta>t}^{High}(\tau)
is defined in Definition
3.3
.

Theorem 1.11

(High density with
\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}
)
.

Fix any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

Fix any three parameters
\tilde{t},\tilde{\beta},s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
0<\tilde{t}<\tilde{\beta}.
Consider the scaling

(1.14)

\beta=\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}},\quad t=\frac{\tilde{t}}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}},\quad\tau=-Nf+(\sigma N)^{1/3}s.

Let
\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}
be defined as in definition
1.9
. There exists
N_{0}\in\Z_{\geq 0}
such that for all
N\geq N_{0}
,
the identity (
1.13
) holds. Then we have the following asymptotic limit:

(1.15)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(\sigma N)^{-1/3}}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}\mathbbm{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}2K_{0}\left(2\sqrt{e^{Nf-(\sigma N)^{1/3}s}\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(N-1,N-1)w}\right)w^{t-\beta-1}e^{-w^{-1}}dw\bigg]=\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}(s)

where
\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}(s)
is defined in Definition
3.5

Theorem 1.12

(High density with
\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}
)
.

Fix any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

Fix any three parameters
\tilde{t},\tilde{\beta},s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}.
Consider the scaling

(1.16)

\beta=\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}},\quad t=\frac{\tilde{t}}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}},\quad\tau=-Nf+\sigma N^{1/3}s.

Let
Z^{t,\beta}
be defined as in definition
1.9
and
\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}
be defined as in Definition
3.5
with a choice of
0<\delta<\tilde{t}
for the contour. Then for any
2\tilde{t}>A>\tilde{t}-\delta

(1.17)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(N-1,N-1)+Nf}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}}\leq s\bigg)

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{1}{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}\partial_{s}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(-A+{\rm i}y)e^{-Ax}e^{{\rm i}xy}\left(\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}(x+s)\right)dxdy.

(High density phase with
-t<\beta<t

.

Theorem 1.14

(High density phase with
-\tilde{t}<\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}

Theorem 1.15

(High density phase with
-\tilde{t}<\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}
)
.

Fix any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}

\tilde{t},\tilde{\beta},s\in\mathbb{R}

\tilde{t}>0,

\tilde{t}>\tilde{\beta}>-\tilde{t}.

(1.21)

t=\frac{\tilde{t}}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}},\quad\beta=\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}},\quad\tau=-Nf+(\sigma N)^{1/3}s

\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}
be defined as in definition
1.9

(1.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(N-1,N-1)+Nf}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}}\leq s\right)=\left(1+\frac{1}{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}\partial_{s}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(A-{\rm i}y)}{2\pi}e^{-Ax}e^{{\rm i}xy}\left(\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}}^{High}(x+s)\right)dxdy.

1.3.
Connection to existing distributions

It is known that positive-temperature models such as the log-gamma polymer can degenerate to zero-temperature models such as exponential last-passage percolation (ELPP). There has been work on the one point distribution of the exponential LPP model with product stationary initial condition, see
[
11
, Theorem 2.7]
. By the KPZ universality, we expect that the asymptotic distribution (
1.12
) in Theorem
1.8
should be the same as
[
11
, Theorem 2.7]
.
However, the central kernel
\widetilde{\mathbb{K}}_{L}
in (
1.10
) is the GSE kernel whereas the central kernel
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
in
[
11
, Theorem 2.7]
is the cross-over kernel. It is not clear why these two formulas should match.

For the two-parameter stationary case, we also expect that the distributions in Theorem
1.12
and Theorem
1.15
should match the one in
[
19
, Theorem 1.6 (1.13)]
. This matching would be significantly harder since formulas in either case are very involved. The differences between kernel decompositions of the log-gamma model and geometric LPP (GLPP) are explained in Section
10
.

It is clear that one may first take the degeneration from the log-gamma model to the exponential LPP model and then tune parameters to obtain stationary ELPP. Alternatively, one may pass from GLPP to ELPP. In either case, it is reasonable to expect that using the shift argument in
[
11
, Lemma 3.3]
and
[
19
, section 6.3.1]
rather than Fourier transform and inversion can equivalently remove extraneous weights at
(1,1)
(for LD) and
(2,1)
(for HD). Consequently, we expect that

(1.23)

\mathbb{P}\left(\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}^{t}(N,N)+Nf}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}}\leq s\right)=\partial_{s}\left(\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}(s)\right),

and similarly,

(1.24)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(N-1,N-1)+Nf}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}}\leq r\right)=\left(\partial_{s}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}\partial_{s}^{2}\right)\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}(s)\quad\text{ when }\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(N-1,N-1)+Nf}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}}\leq r\right)=\left(\partial_{s}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}\partial_{s}^{2}\right)\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}}^{High}(s)\quad\text{ when }\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t},

where
\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low},

\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High},

\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}}^{High}

are defined in Definition
3.2
, Definition
3.5
, and Definition
3.8
.

We formulate the above observation as the following conjecture.

Conjecture 1.16
.

Let
\tilde{\beta},

\tilde{t}
,
\alpha,

A
be defined as in Theorem
1.15
and let
\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}}^{High}
be defined as in Definition
3.8
.
We expect the following identities of functions:

(1.25)

\left(1+\frac{1}{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}\partial_{s}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(A-{\rm i}y)e^{-Ax}e^{{\rm i}xy}\left(\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}}^{High}(x+v)\right)dxdy=\widetilde{F}_{-\tilde{\beta},-\tilde{t}}^{HD}(v),

where
\widetilde{F}_{-\tilde{\beta},-\tilde{t}}^{HD}
is defined in
[
19
, Theorem 1.6 (1.13)]
.

Conjecture 1.17
.

Let
\tilde{\beta},

\tilde{t}
,
\alpha,

A
be defined as in Theorem
1.12
and let
\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}
be defined as in Definition
3.5
. We expect the following identities of functions:

(1.26)

\left(1+\frac{1}{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}\partial_{s}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(A-{\rm i}y)e^{-Ax}e^{{\rm i}xy}\left(\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}(x+v)\right)dxdy=\widetilde{F}_{-\tilde{\beta},-\tilde{t}}^{HD}(v),

where
\widetilde{F}_{-\tilde{\beta},-\tilde{t}}^{HD}
is defined in
[
19
, Theorem 1.6 (1.13)]
.

Conjecture 1.18
.

Let
\tilde{\beta},

\tilde{t}
,
\alpha,

A
be defined as in Theorem
1.8
and let
\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}
be defined as in Definition
3.2
. We expect the following identities of functions:

(1.27)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(-A+{\rm i}y)e^{-Ax}e^{{\rm i}xy}\left(\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}(x+v)\right)dxdy=\widetilde{F}_{-\tilde{\beta}}^{LD}(v)=F_{0,half}^{(\tilde{\beta},0)}(v),

where
\widetilde{F}_{-\tilde{\beta}}^{LD}
is defined in

and
F_{0,half}^{(\tilde{\beta},0)}
is defined in

, Theorem 2.7]
.

1.4.
Our method and novelty

Our goal is to derive the one-point asymptotic distribution of the half-space log-gamma polymer with stationary initial condition.
The two-parameter stationary measure of the log-gamma polymer can be scaled to obtain the stationary measure of the half-space KPZ equation, as proved in
[
5
, Theorem 1.4]
.
Applying scaling to this stationary measure then yields the stationary measure for the half-space KPZ fixed point, see
[
7
, Section 4.2]
. Our result should imply the one-point distribution of half-space KPZ fixed point with stationary initial condition.

To access this universal distribution, we start with a Fredholm Pfaffian algebraic structure of the half-space log-gamma polymer described in
[
17
, Theorem 5.10]
.
We specialize the diagonal, first-row, and second-row parameters so that the first two rows reproduce the exact two-parameter stationary measure, and then apply the critical scaling to obtain asymptotics. The main technical challenge is the tuning of parameters. While half-space log-gamma models are typically defined with positive parameters, achieving stationary initial data requires tuning certain parameters to negative values, which causes the Fredholm Pfaffian to diverge. To address this, we decompose the kernel to extract the divergent contributions.

Such kernel decompositions have appeared previously; see
[
11
, Section 3.2.3]
and
[
19
, Section 6.6]
.
Our decomposition in the product stationary case follows similar ideas (see Section
6.2
).
However, in the two-parameter setting the decomposition is substantially more involved, essentially because the log-gamma kernel in
[
17
, Theorem 5.10]
is symmetric in the diagonal, first-row, and second-row parameters.
In particular, in the High density regime
0>\beta>-t
, both the diagonal parameter
\beta
and the first-row parameter must be tuned to negative values.
We therefore need to extract these poles simultaneously and reorganize them so that the parameters can be analytically continued while the Fredholm Pfaffian of the remaining kernel still converges.
A detailed comparison between our decomposition and earlier ones is given in Section
10
.
This new decomposition is necessary for recovering the full High density regime.

Our next goal is to translate our limiting formulas (
1.8
), (
1.13
), (
1.20
), which involve expectations of Bessel function integrals, into exact distribution formulas. In the LPP setting, this issue is resolved by performing a shift argument as described in (
1.23
) and (
1.24
). In the log-gamma case, we need to use the shift argument (for High density regime) together with the fourier inversion. This step requires uniform control over both the upper and lower tails of the scaled free energy. We note that these tail estimates may be of independent probabilistic interest. Accordingly, we provide the first exponential upper bounds for the upper and lower tail of the scaled free energy for the half-space stationary log-gamma model.

Our strategy is to transport existing tail estimates from the full-space to the half-space geometry. This is facilitated by a distributional correspondence
9
that identifies the half-space point-to-line partition function with the full-space point-to-point partition function under specific parameter correspondence rules. To employ the distributional identity, one first need to embed the half-space model to a trapezoid model with specific parameter choices. Because stationary models have negative parameters that will be sent to zero under critical scaling, if one naively maps the half-space stationary model to the trapezoid model, either the trapezoid model becomes undefined or no longer exhibits the desired lower tails. To resolve this, we use the monotonicity of the inverse-gamma random variable to construct a sequence of intermediate models to transport the existing tails. However, the derivation of these bounds still entails distinct challenges:

(1)

The upper tail
: The stationary models are stochastically larger than its non-stationary counterparts, with the two-parameter stationary model being even larger than the product-stationary model. This hierarchy and the negativity of the parameters make it difficult to identify a dominating model that can be properly embedded to the trapezoid model. Instead, we separate the two-parameter stationary model into two components and establish control over each part. Furthermore, tail bounds for the inhomogeneous full-space log-gamma polymer are not available in the literature. While one could theoretically extract exponential upper bound of order
e^{-cx^{3/2}}
for upper tails of inhomogeneous full-space model using the same involved steepest descent analysis as in
3
, such approach will yield an exponentially decaying error term like
e^{-cN}
. In contrast, we provide a more direct steepest descent analysis using the kernel from
17
with much nicer contours and obtain a clean exponential bound that avoids this extra error term. One might expect that the asymptotic formulas (
1.10
), (
1.15
), and (
1.18
) would provide information about the upper tail of the model.
In the LPP setting, the upper tail can indeed be read off from such formulas, since the shift argument reduces to differentiation, and differentiating an exponentially decaying tail preserves exponential decay.
In contrast, in the log-gamma case, applying Fourier inversion to the asymptotics obscures the known exponential upper-tail decay of the formula.

(2)

The lower tail
: Unlike the upper tail, using the homogeneous half-space model as an intermediate model for the lower tail is straightforward. The difficulty here arises from the nature of the correspondence between half-space and full-space models. Since we want the lower tail of the point-to-point partition function, knowing the point-to-line partition function, which is larger than the point-to-point partition function, seems insufficient. We resolve this by leveraging the stationarity of the model, which induces a random walk behavior with a negative drift along the down-right line. This drift ensures that the point-to-line sum is dominated by the partition function near the diagonal. Using refined random walk estimates, we are able to translate the lower tail of full-space homogeneous point-to-point partition function to the lower tail of half-space partition function on the diagonal. Our method can also derive the lower tail of point-to-point partition function of stationary model away from the diagonal.

While we expect the optimal upper and lower tail decay to be of the form
e^{-cx^{3/2}}
and
e^{-cx^{3}}
as in the case of the full-space model, we focus here on establishing the exponential bounds (linear in
x
in the exponent) with no
N
-dependent error terms.

1.5.
Organization of Paper

Sections 2-5 introduce the notations, define the functions appearing in the main results, and introduce the models and their Pfaffian structures. Sections 6-8 are devoted to the reformulation of the kernel, the analytic continuation of the Fredholm Pfaffian formula, and the asymptotic analysis in the product-stationary and two-parameter stationary cases when
\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}
. Analogously, Sections 9, 11, and 12 treat the two-parameter stationary case when
\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}
, including the kernel decomposition, analytic continuation, and asymptotic analysis. Section 10 highlights the differences and new features in the kernel decomposition introduced in Section 9. Sections 13–14 establish lower and upper tail estimates for the half-space stationary models. Sections 15–16 provide the lower and upper tail bounds required for the Fourier inversion argument. Finally, Section 17 presents numerical evaluations of the stationary log-gamma polymer and exponential LPP.

Figure 2
.
This picture describes the phase diagram of the half-space stationary log-gamma polymer model under critical scaling, which sets
t=\tilde{t}/(\sigma N)^{1/3}
and
\beta=\tilde{\beta}/(\sigma N)^{1/3}.
We characterize the distribution of
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\log Z(N,N)+Nf}{(\sigma N)^{1/3}}
under three different cases. Under the High density phase, when
\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}
, we have
\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}
and when
-\tilde{t}<\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}
, we have
\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}}^{High}
as limiting distributions. Under the Low density phase, we obtain
\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}
as the limiting distribution. In particular, we have
\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}
on the line
\tilde{\beta}=\tilde{t}
,
\tilde{t}>0
in the High density regime.

2.
Basic notations

In this paper, we will have two kinds of limits. We consistently use
\widehat{F}
,
{F}^{L}
(respectively) to denote the limit
\lim_{u\rightarrow-t}F(t)
in High density and Low density regimes, respectively. We consistently use
\widetilde{F}
,
\widetilde{F}^{L}
to denote the asymptotic limit of
\widehat{F}
,
{F}^{L}
under the critical scaling in the High density and Low density regimes, respectively.

We use the bracket notation to denote the scalar product on
\mathbb{L}^{2}((\tau,\infty))
, that is

(2.1)

\displaystyle\left\langle f\left|g\right\rangle\right.=\left\langle f(x)\left|g(x)\right\rangle\right.=\int_{\tau}^{\infty}f(x)g(x)dx,

\displaystyle\left\langle f\left|h\right|g\right\rangle=\left\langle f(x)\left|h(x,y)\right|g(y)\right\rangle=\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}f(x)h(x,y)g(y)dxdy,

\displaystyle K\left|g\right\rangle(x)=\int_{\tau}^{\infty}K(x,y)g(y)dy.

We use
\left|f\right\rangle\left\langle g\right|
to denote the outer product kernel

(2.2)

\left|f\right\rangle\left\langle g\right|(x,y)=f(x)g(y).

We will remove the parameters
(x,y)
when the context is clear.
We define the contour

(2.3)

\displaystyle C(\delta;\theta)=\{\delta+re^{\text{sign}(r)\theta}:r\in\mathbb{R}\},

where the choice of
\delta
will be specified for each function.
We assume the positive orientation of the contour.

Throughout this paper, we define
K_{\nu}(z)
to be the modified Bessel function as in
1
.

3.
Definition of functions

Definition 3.1

(Low density phase)
.

Fix any two parameters
t,\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
satisfying
t\in(0,0.5)
and
\alpha>t.
We define

(3.1)

\displaystyle Q_{N,t}^{Low}(\tau):=\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{L}\right)\left(\widehat{C}_{L}-1\right)+\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{L}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{L}\\
-\widehat{\phi}_{1}^{L}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widehat{B}_{L}\quad-\widehat{B}_{L}^{\prime}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{B}_{L}\\
-\widehat{B}_{L}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{L}\quad\widehat{\phi}_{1}^{L}\right|\right),

where Fredholm Pfaffian is taken over
\mathbb{L}^{2}((\tau,\infty))
.

Let
{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d
denote a vertical line going from
-d-{\rm i}\infty
to
-d+{\rm i}\infty
with
0<d<t
and
\frac{1}{N}<d
. We omit the dependence of
Q_{N,t}^{Low}(\tau)
on
d
.
We define all functions used in the above definition.

(3.2)

\displaystyle G_{\alpha}(X)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+X)}{\Gamma(\alpha-X)}\right)^{N-1},\quad Q(Z,W):=\Gamma(-2Z)\Gamma(-2W)\frac{\sin(\pi(Z-W))}{\sin(\pi(Z+W))},

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}_{1}^{L}(X)

\displaystyle:=-\Gamma(2t)G_{\alpha}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}Ze^{XZ}e^{\tau(t+W)}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(t+Z)}{\Gamma(t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W+1)}{\Gamma(-t-W+1)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}G_{\alpha}(Z)G_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W),

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}_{2}^{L}(X)

\displaystyle:=-\Gamma(2t)G_{\alpha}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{XZ}e^{\tau(t+W)}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(t+Z)}{\Gamma(t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W+1)}{\Gamma(-t-W+1)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}G_{\alpha}(Z)G_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W),

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{L}(X)

\displaystyle:=\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{XW}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(1+t+W)}{\Gamma(1+t-W)}G_{\alpha}(W)\Gamma(-2W),

\displaystyle\widehat{B}^{\prime}_{L}(X)

\displaystyle:=\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}We^{XW}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(1+t+W)}{\Gamma(1+t-W)}G_{\alpha}(W)\Gamma(-2W),

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{L}

\displaystyle:=G_{\alpha}(t)\Gamma(2t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{\tau(t+W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)^{2}}{\Gamma(t-W)^{2}}G_{\alpha}(W)\Gamma(-2W).

(3.3)

\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{L}(X,Y):=\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{K}_{L}(X,Y)&-\partial_{Y}\widehat{K}_{L}(X,Y)\\
-\partial_{X}\widehat{K}_{L}(X,Y)&\partial_{X}\partial_{Y}\widehat{K}_{L}(X,Y)\end{pmatrix}

with

(3.4)

\begin{split}{\widehat{K}_{L}}(X,Y):=&\int\limits_{\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}-d_{0}}\frac{dZ}{2\pi i}\int\limits_{\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}-d_{0}}\frac{dW}{2\pi i}\;e^{XZ+YW}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(t+Z)}{\Gamma(t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}\\
&G_{\alpha}(Z)\,G_{\alpha}(W)\,Q(Z,W).\end{split}

Definition 3.2

(Low density phase)
.

Fix any
\alpha,\tilde{t}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. We define

(3.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}(s)

\displaystyle:=\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\mathbb{K}}_{L}\right)\left(\widetilde{C}_{L}-1\right)+\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\mathbb{K}}_{L}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\phi}_{2}^{L}\\
-\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{L}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widetilde{B}_{L}\quad-\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{L}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{B}_{L}\\
-\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{L}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widetilde{\phi}_{2}^{L}\quad\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{L}\right|\right)

where Fredholm Pfaffian is taken over
\mathbb{L}^{2}((s,\infty)).

Choose
\tilde{t}>\delta>0
. We omit the dependence of
\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{t}}^{Low}
on
\delta
.
We define all functions used in the above definition.

(3.6)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{L}:=

\displaystyle-\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{W}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{t}}\right)\frac{(\tilde{W}+\tilde{t})}{4(\tilde{W}-\tilde{t})^{2}}e^{-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{L}(v):=

\displaystyle-\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{2\tilde{W}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{W}},\quad\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{L}(v)=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{2(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{W}}.

(3.7)

\widetilde{\mathbb{K}}_{L}(u,v):=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{K}_{L}(u,v)&-\partial_{v}\widetilde{K}_{L}(u,v)\\
-\partial_{u}\widetilde{K}_{L}(u,v)&\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\widetilde{K}_{L}(u,v),\end{pmatrix}

where

(3.8)

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{L}(u,v):=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{4\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{W}}.

(3.9)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{L}(u):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{t}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{2}^{L}(u):=

\displaystyle-\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{t}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}}.

\displaystyle{Q}_{N,\beta>t}^{High}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\mathbb{K}}\right)\left(\widehat{C}-1\right)+\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\mathbb{K}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\phi}_{2}\\
-\widehat{\phi}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widehat{B}\quad-\widehat{B}^{\prime}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{B}\\
-\widehat{B}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widehat{\phi}_{2}\quad\widehat{\phi}_{1}\right|\right)

where Fredholm Pfaffian is taken over
\mathbb{L}^{2}((\tau,\infty))
.

Let
{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d
denote a vertical line going from
-d-{\rm i}\infty
to

with
0<d<t
and
\frac{1}{N}<d

on
\delta
.
We define all functions used in the above theorem.

(3.11)

\displaystyle G_{\alpha,\beta}(X)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{\Gamma(\beta+X)}{\Gamma(\beta-X)}\right)\left(\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+X)}{\Gamma(\alpha-X)}\right)^{N-2},\quad Q(Z,W):=\Gamma(-2Z)\Gamma(-2W)\frac{\sin(\pi(Z-W))}{\sin(\pi(Z+W))},

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}_{1}(X)

\displaystyle:=-\Gamma(2t)G_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}Ze^{XZ}e^{\tau(t+W)}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(t+Z)}{\Gamma(t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W+1)}{\Gamma(-t-W+1)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}G_{\alpha,\beta}(Z)G_{\alpha,\beta}(W)Q(Z,W),

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}_{2}(X)

\displaystyle:=-\Gamma(2t)G_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{XZ}e^{\tau(t+W)}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(t+Z)}{\Gamma(t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W+1)}{\Gamma(-t-W+1)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}G_{\alpha,\beta}(Z)G_{\alpha,\beta}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.12)

\displaystyle\widehat{B}(X)

\displaystyle:=\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{XW}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(1+t+W)}{\Gamma(1+t-W)}G_{\alpha,\beta}(W)\Gamma(-2W),

\displaystyle\widehat{B}^{\prime}(X)

\displaystyle:=\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}We^{XW}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(1+t+W)}{\Gamma(1+t-W)}G_{\alpha,\beta}(W)\Gamma(-2W),

\displaystyle\widehat{C}

\displaystyle:=G_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\Gamma(2t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{\tau(t+W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)^{2}}{\Gamma(t-W)^{2}}G_{\alpha,\beta}(W)\Gamma(-2W).

(3.13)

\widehat{\mathbb{K}}(X,Y):=\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{K}(X,Y)&-\partial_{Y}\widehat{K}(X,Y)\\
-\partial_{X}\widehat{K}(X,Y)&\partial_{X}\partial_{Y}\widehat{K}(X,Y)\end{pmatrix}

with

(3.14)

\begin{split}{\widehat{K}}(X,Y):=&\int\limits_{\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}-d_{0}}\frac{dZ}{2\pi i}\int\limits_{\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}-d_{0}}\frac{dW}{2\pi i}\;e^{XZ+YW}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(t+Z)}{\Gamma(t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}\\
&G_{\alpha,\beta}(Z)\,G_{\alpha,\beta}(W)\,Q(Z,W).\end{split}

Remark 3.4
.

One may ask whether the product stationary case can be obtained by taking the limit
\beta\to t
in the above formula, since the two-parameter stationary initial condition then degenerates to the product stationary one. However, two obstacles arise.
First, Definition
1.9
introduces a random shift at
(2,2)
through
\mathcal{Z}^{t,\beta}(1,1)=X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\Gamma(\beta+t)
; under critical scaling this contribution remains nontrivial, leading to a worse result than the direct Low density formula
\widehat{Q}_{N,t}^{Low}
.
Second, setting
\beta=t
at finite
N
forces

A=e^{-Xu}\left(\frac{\Gamma(t-u)}{\Gamma(t+u)}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\Gamma(\alpha-u)}{\Gamma(\alpha+u)}\right)^{N-2},

which is defined in (
6.5
).
The denominator of
A
contains
\Gamma(t+u)^{2}
. This factor cannot be simply compensated by removing the
(1,1)
weight (which produces only one
\Gamma(t+u)
factor), making the computation substantially more difficult.
Finally, the Maximal current phase cannot be recovered by taking
t\to 1
, essentially due to the symmetry of the kernel.

Definition 3.5

(High density with
\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}
)
.

Fix any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

Fix any three parameters
\tilde{t},\tilde{\beta},s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
0<\tilde{t}<\tilde{\beta}.
We define

(3.15)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}(s)

\displaystyle:=\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\mathbb{K}}\right)\left(\widetilde{C}-1\right)+\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\mathbb{K}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\phi}_{2}\\
-\widetilde{\phi}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widetilde{B}\quad-\widetilde{B}^{\prime}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{B}\\
-\widetilde{B}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widetilde{\phi}_{2}\quad\widetilde{\phi}_{1}\right|\right)

where Fredholm Pfaffian is taken over
\mathbb{L}^{2}((s,\infty)).

We define all functions used in the above theorem. The dependence of
\widetilde{Q}_{\tilde{\beta}>\tilde{t}}^{High}
on
\delta
is omitted.

(3.16)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}:=

\displaystyle-\frac{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}{\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{W}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{t}}\right)\frac{(\tilde{W}+\tilde{t})}{4(\tilde{W}-\tilde{t})^{2}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}e^{-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}(v):=

\displaystyle-\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{2\tilde{W}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{W}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}^{\prime}(v):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{2(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{W}}.

(3.17)

\widetilde{\mathbb{K}}(u,v):=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{K}(u,v)&-\partial_{v}\widetilde{K}(u,v)\\
-\partial_{u}\widetilde{K}(u,v)&\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\widetilde{K}(u,v)\end{pmatrix},

where

(3.18)

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}(u,v):=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{4\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{W}}.

(3.19)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{1}(u):=

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}{\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{t}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{2}(u):=

\displaystyle-\frac{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}{\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{t}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}}.

Definition 3.6

(High density phase with
-t<\beta<t
)
.

Fix any three parameters
t,\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
t\in(0,0.5),

\beta\in(-t,t)
, and
\alpha>t.
We define

(3.20)

\displaystyle{Q}_{N,\beta<t}^{High}(\tau)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}\widehat{\NN}-\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}+\widehat{\mathcal{B}}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\right).

The Fredholm Pfaffian is taken over
\mathbb{L}^{2}((\tau,\infty))
.

Let
{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d
denote a vertical line going from
-d-{\rm i}\infty
to
-d+{\rm i}\infty
with
\beta<d<t
and
\frac{1}{N}<d
. We omit the dependence of
{Q}_{N,\beta<t}^{High}
on
\delta
.
We define each function used in the above theorem. The well-definedness of
\frac{1}{\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)}
is explained in the proof of Theorem
1.13
.

(3.21)

H_{\alpha}(Z):=\left(\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+Z)}{\Gamma(\alpha-Z)}\right)^{N-2}\!\!\!\!,\quad F(W):=\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(\beta+W)}{\Gamma(\beta-W)},\quad Q(Z,W):=\Gamma(-2Z)\Gamma(-2W)\frac{\sin(\pi(Z-W))}{\sin(\pi(Z+W))}.

(3.22)

\displaystyle c_{0}:=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t-\beta)}{\Gamma(-t+\beta)}\frac{\Gamma(t-\beta)}{\Gamma(t+\beta)}H_{\alpha}(-\beta),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1}:=

\displaystyle c_{0}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{-(\beta+W)}{(\beta-W)}e^{-\tau(\beta-W)}\frac{\Gamma(1+t+W)}{\Gamma(1+t-W)}\Gamma(-2W)F(W)H_{\alpha}(W),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2}:=

\displaystyle c_{0}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dU}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(Z-U)(W+\beta)}{(Z+U)(\beta-W)}e^{\tau(U+Z+W-\beta)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)\Gamma(-t-W)}

\displaystyle F(U)H_{\alpha}(U)\Gamma(-2U)\frac{\Gamma(1+t+U)}{\Gamma(1+t-U)}F(Z)H_{\alpha}(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.23)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{1}:=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(W-Z)}{(Z+W)}e^{\tau(Z+W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+Z)}{\Gamma(t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+Z)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(1+t+W)}{\Gamma(1+t-W)}

\displaystyle\Gamma(-2Z)\Gamma(-2W)F(W)H_{\alpha}(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)+\frac{\Gamma(\beta-t)}{\Gamma(\beta+t)}\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+t)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-t)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{2}:=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dU}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dV}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(Z-U)(V-W)}{(Z+U)(V+W)}e^{\tau(Z+U+V+W)}F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+V)}{\Gamma(t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+V)}{\Gamma(-t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+V)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-V)}\frac{\Gamma(1+t+U)}{\Gamma(1+t-U)}\Gamma(-2V)\Gamma(-2U)F(U)H_{\alpha}(U)H_{\alpha}(V).

(3.24)

\displaystyle c_{1}:=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t-\beta)}{\Gamma(-t+\beta)}\frac{\Gamma(t-\beta)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}\Gamma(2t)H_{\alpha}(-\beta)H_{\alpha}(t),\quad\mathcal{C}_{1}:=c_{1}\frac{(\beta+t)}{(\beta-t)}e^{-\tau(\beta-t)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}:=

\displaystyle c_{1}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(Z-t)(\beta+W)}{(t+Z)(W-\beta)}e^{\tau(t+Z-\beta+W)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}:=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{W-t}{W+t}e^{\tau(t+W)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}\Gamma(-2W)\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+W)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-W)}\Gamma(2t)\frac{\Gamma(\beta+t)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}H_{\alpha}(t)H_{\alpha}(W),

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}:=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dV}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(Z-t)(W-V)}{(Z+t)(V+W)}e^{\tau(V+W+Z+t)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+V)}{\Gamma(t-V)}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t+V)}{\Gamma(-t-V)}H_{\alpha}(V)\Gamma(-2V)\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+V)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-V)}\Gamma(2t)\frac{\Gamma(\beta+t)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}H_{\alpha}(t)F(Z)H_{\alpha}(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}:=

\displaystyle\Gamma(2t)\frac{\Gamma(\beta+t)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}H_{\alpha}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{W-t}{-(W+t)}e^{\tau(t+W)}\frac{\Gamma(1+t+W)}{\Gamma(1+t-W)}\Gamma(-2W)F(W)H_{\alpha}(W),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{2}:=

\displaystyle\Gamma(2t)\frac{\Gamma(\beta+t)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}H_{\alpha}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dV}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(Z-V)(W-t)}{(W+t)(V+Z)}e^{\tau(t+W+V+Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}

\displaystyle\Gamma(-2V)\frac{\Gamma(1+t+V)}{\Gamma(1-t-V)}F(V)H_{\alpha}(V)F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.27)

\displaystyle\NN_{1}:=

\displaystyle 1+c_{0}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}H_{\alpha}(W)\Gamma(-2W)\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+W)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-W)}\frac{\beta+W}{\beta-W}e^{-\tau(\beta-W)},

\displaystyle\NN_{2}:=

\displaystyle c_{0}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dV}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\beta+W)(Z-V)}{(W-\beta)(V+Z)}e^{-\tau(\beta-W-V-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+V)}{\Gamma(-t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(t+V)}{\Gamma(t-V)}H_{\alpha}(V)\Gamma(-2V)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+V)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-V)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.28)

\displaystyle\widehat{\aleph}_{1}(X):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dU}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(U-Z)}{(U+Z)}e^{\tau(Z+U)+XW}F(U)H_{\alpha}(U)\Gamma(-2U)\frac{\Gamma(1+t+U)}{\Gamma(1+t-U)}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W),

\displaystyle\widehat{\aleph}_{2}(X):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dU}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{-(U-Z)W}{(U+Z)}e^{\tau(Z+U)+XW}F(U)H_{\alpha}(U)\Gamma(-2U)\frac{\Gamma(1+t+U)}{\Gamma(1+t-U)}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W),

(3.29)

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{1}(X):=

\displaystyle\Gamma(2t)\frac{\Gamma(\beta+t)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}H_{\alpha}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(Z-t)}{(Z+t)}e^{\tau(Z+t)+XW}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}

\displaystyle F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W),

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{2}(X):=

\displaystyle\Gamma(2t)\frac{\Gamma(\beta+t)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}H_{\alpha}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{-(Z-t)W}{(Z+t)}e^{\tau(Z+t)+XW}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}

\displaystyle F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.30)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Theta}_{1}(X):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dV}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(V-Z)}{(V+Z)}e^{\tau(Z+V)+XW}\frac{\Gamma(-t+V)}{\Gamma(-t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(t+V)}{\Gamma(t-V)}H_{\alpha}(V)\Gamma(-2V)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+V)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-V)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}F(Z)H_{\alpha}(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Theta}_{2}(X):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dV}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{W(Z-V)}{V+Z}e^{\tau(Z+V)+XW}\frac{\Gamma(-t+V)}{\Gamma(-t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(t+V)}{\Gamma(t-V)}H_{\alpha}(V)\Gamma(-2V)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+V)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-V)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}F(Z)H_{\alpha}(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.31)

\displaystyle\widehat{\zeta}_{1}(Y):=

\displaystyle\Gamma(2t)\frac{\Gamma(\beta+t)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}H_{\alpha}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{Z(W-t)}{(W+t)}e^{\tau(t+W)+YZ}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}

\displaystyle F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W),

\displaystyle\widehat{\zeta}_{2}(Y):=

\displaystyle\Gamma(2t)\frac{\Gamma(\beta+t)}{\Gamma(\beta-t)}H_{\alpha}(t)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(W-t)}{(W+t)}e^{\tau(t+W)+YZ}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}

\displaystyle F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.32)

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}_{1}(Y):=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(t-\beta)}{\Gamma(t+\beta)}\frac{\Gamma(-t-\beta)}{\Gamma(-t+\beta)}H_{\alpha}(-\beta)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{Z(W+\beta)}{(W-\beta)}e^{-\tau(\beta-W)+YZ}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}

\displaystyle F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W),

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}_{2}(Y):=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(t-\beta)}{\Gamma(t+\beta)}\frac{\Gamma(-t-\beta)}{\Gamma(-t+\beta)}H_{\alpha}(-\beta)\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(W+\beta)}{(W-\beta)}e^{-\tau(\beta-W)+YZ}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}

\displaystyle F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W).

(3.33)

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{1}(Y):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\!\!\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\!\!\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\!\!\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\!\!\frac{dV}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(W-V)Z}{(V+W)}e^{\tau(V+W)+YZ}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(t+V)}{\Gamma(t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+V)}{\Gamma(-t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+V)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-V)}\Gamma(-2V)H_{\alpha}(V),

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{2}(Y):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\!\!\frac{dZ}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\!\!\frac{dW}{2\pi{\rm i}}\!\!\int\limits_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}-d}\!\!\frac{dV}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(W-V)}{(V+W)}e^{\tau(V+W)+YZ}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}F(Z)F(W)H_{\alpha}(Z)H_{\alpha}(W)Q(Z,W)

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(t+V)}{\Gamma(t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+V)}{\Gamma(-t-V)}\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta+V)}{\Gamma(1-\beta-V)}\Gamma(-2V)H_{\alpha}(V).

Define the kernel

(3.34)

\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(X,Y):=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{K}(X,Y)&-\partial_{X}\mathcal{K}(X,Y)\\
-\partial_{Y}\mathcal{K}(X,Y)&\partial_{X}\partial_{Y}\mathcal{K}(X,Y)\end{pmatrix},

with

(3.35)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}(X,Y):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}-d_{0}}\frac{dZ}{2\pi i}\int\limits_{\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}-d_{0}}\frac{dW}{2\pi i}\;e^{XZ+YW}\frac{\Gamma(-t+Z)}{\Gamma(-t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(t+Z)}{\Gamma(t-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(-t+W)}{\Gamma(-t-W)}\frac{\Gamma(t+W)}{\Gamma(t-W)}

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(\beta+Z)}{\Gamma(\beta-Z)}\frac{\Gamma(\beta+W)}{\Gamma(\beta-W)}H_{\alpha}(Z)\,H_{\alpha}(W)\,Q(Z,W).

(3.36)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\mathcal{A}_{1}+\mathcal{A}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\eta}_{2}\\
-\widehat{\eta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widehat{\aleph}_{1}\quad\widehat{\aleph}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\aleph}_{1}\\
\widehat{\aleph}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widehat{\eta}_{2}\quad\widehat{\eta}_{1}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\mathcal{B}_{1}+\mathcal{B}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\theta}_{2}\\
-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widehat{\aleph}_{1}\quad\widehat{\aleph}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\aleph}_{1}\\
\widehat{\aleph}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widehat{\theta}_{2}\quad\widehat{\theta}_{1}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\mathcal{C}_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\eta}_{2}\\
-\widehat{\eta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widehat{\psi}_{1}\quad\widehat{\psi}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\psi}_{1}\\
\widehat{\psi}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widehat{\eta}_{2}\quad\widehat{\eta}_{1}\right|\right),

(3.37)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{D}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\theta}_{2}\\
-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widehat{\psi}_{1}\quad\widehat{\psi}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\psi}_{1}\\
\widehat{\psi}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widehat{\theta}_{2}\quad\widehat{\theta}_{1}\right|\right),

(3.38)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{M}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}+\mathcal{M}_{2}-1\right)+\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\zeta}_{2}\\
-\widehat{\zeta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widehat{\aleph}_{1}\quad\widehat{\aleph}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\aleph}_{1}\\
\widehat{\aleph}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widehat{\zeta}_{2}\quad\widehat{\zeta}_{1}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{\NN}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\NN_{1}+\NN_{2}-1\right)+\Pf\left(J-\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\eta}_{2}\\
-\widehat{\eta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widehat{\Theta}_{1}\quad\widehat{\Theta}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\Theta}_{1}\\
\widehat{\Theta}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widehat{\eta}_{2}\quad\widehat{\eta}_{1}\right|\right).

Remark 3.7
.

One may also ask what happens to our formula in the special cases
\beta=t
and
\beta=-t
. We discuss them separately.

When
\beta=-t
, the log-gamma polymer model explodes due to the inverse-Gamma random weight at
(2,2)
. Thus, our formula also diverges.
When
\beta=t
, our kernel decomposition breaks down. In the kernel decomposition in section
9.1
, we removed the poles at
Z=-\beta
and
W=-\beta
, but if
\beta=t
these poles should instead remain inside the double contours and merge into a double pole at
t
. Consequently, the decomposition of
\bm{\check{\mathcal{K}}}
into
X_{i}
and
Y_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
is no longer valid, and all terms must be recomputed and regrouped to extract the limit.
Moreover, there remains a random shift at
(2,2)
. Although the two-parameter stationary condition degenerates to the product stationary case at
\beta=t
, the partition function still retains this random shift, whose contribution is nontrivial under critical scaling. As a result, the outcome is worse than the Low density formula
\widehat{Q}_{N,t}^{\mathrm{Low}}
.

Definition 3.8

(High density phase with
-\tilde{t}<\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}
)
.

Fix any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and any three parameters
\tilde{t},\tilde{\beta},s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\tilde{t}>0,

\tilde{t}>\tilde{\beta}>-\tilde{t}.
We define

(3.39)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}}^{High}(s)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\widetilde{\NN}-\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}\right).

The Fredholm Pfaffian is taken over
\mathbb{L}^{2}((s,\infty))
.

We choose
\delta
satisfying
\tilde{\beta}<\delta<\tilde{t}
. The contours for all functions defined below are
C(\delta;\pi/3)
. The dependence of
\widetilde{{Q}}_{\tilde{\beta}<\tilde{t}}^{High}
on
\delta
is omitted. The well-definedness of
\frac{1}{\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)}
is explained in the proof of Theorem
1.14
.

(3.40)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}:

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})}{2\tilde{W}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{\beta}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}:

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{U}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{U}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{t}+\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{U})}{(\tilde{U}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{t}-\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{U})}

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{8\tilde{U}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}e^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{\beta}+\frac{\tilde{U}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{U}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}.

(3.41)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}:

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{W}-\tilde{Z})}{4\tilde{W}\tilde{Z}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{W}+\tilde{Z})}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}+\frac{\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t}}{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2}:

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{U}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{V}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{U})(\tilde{t}+\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{U})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{U})(\tilde{t}-\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{U})}

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{V}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{16\tilde{V}\tilde{U}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{V}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{U}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{U}+\frac{\tilde{V}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{V}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}

(3.42)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{1}:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tilde{t}}e^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{\beta}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2}:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tilde{t}}\frac{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}{\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{4\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{\beta}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}}

(3.43)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1}:

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t})}{4\tilde{W}\tilde{t}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t})}e^{-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{2}:

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{V}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{W}-\tilde{V})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{t}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{W}+\tilde{V})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t})}{16\tilde{t}\tilde{V}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t})}e^{\frac{\tilde{V}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{V}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}

(3.44)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}:=

\displaystyle-\frac{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t})}{2\tilde{t}(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t})}e^{-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}\frac{(\tilde{W}+\tilde{t})^{2}}{(\tilde{W}-\tilde{t})^{2}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{2\tilde{W}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{2}:=

\displaystyle-\frac{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t})}{2\tilde{t}(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t})}e^{-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{V}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{V}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{W}+\tilde{t})}{(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{V}+\tilde{Z})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{V}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{V}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{V})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{V})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{V})}{(\tilde{t}-\tilde{V})}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{8\tilde{V}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}}

(3.45)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\NN}_{1}:=

\displaystyle 1-e^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{\beta}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{2\tilde{W}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\NN}_{2}:=

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{\beta}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{V}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}+\frac{\tilde{V}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{V}}

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{V})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{V})}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{8\tilde{Z}\tilde{V}\tilde{W}}.

The following:

(3.46)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\aleph}_{1}(u):=-

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{U}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{U}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{t}+\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{U}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{t}-\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{U}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{U}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{U}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{W}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\aleph}_{2}(u):=-

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{U}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{U}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{t}+\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{U}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{t}-\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{U})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{U}\tilde{Z}(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{U}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{U}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{W}}

(3.47)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}_{1}(u):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{t})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{t}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{t})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{W}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}_{2}(u):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{t})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{t}\tilde{Z}(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{t})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{W}}

(3.48)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Theta}_{1}(u):=-

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{V}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{V}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{V}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{V}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{V}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{V}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{W}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Theta}_{2}(u):=-

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{V}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{V}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{V}\tilde{Z}(\tilde{V}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{V}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{V}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{W}}

(3.49)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}(v):=

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}{\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{t}\tilde{W}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{Z}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{2}(v):=-

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{t}}{\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{t}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{t}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{8\tilde{t}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{Z}-\frac{\tilde{t}^{3}}{3}+s\tilde{t}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}

(3.50)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}_{1}(v):=

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{\beta}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{\tilde{Z}(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{W}+\tilde{\beta})}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{4\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}_{2}(v):=

\displaystyle-e^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{\beta}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{W}+\tilde{\beta})}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{4\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}},

(3.51)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\theta}_{1}(v):=

\displaystyle-\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{V}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{\tilde{Z}(\tilde{V}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{V}+\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{V}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{V}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{Z}}

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{8\tilde{V}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\theta}_{2}(v):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{V}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\frac{(\tilde{V}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{V}+\tilde{W})}e^{\frac{\tilde{V}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{V}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-s\tilde{W}+\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{Z}}

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{8\tilde{V}\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}}.

Define the kernel

(3.52)

\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(u,v):=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(u,v)&-\partial_{v}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(u,v)\\
-\partial_{u}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(u,v)&\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}(u,v)\end{pmatrix},

with

(3.53)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{K}}(u,v):=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{Z}}{2\pi i}\int\limits_{C(\delta;\pi/3)}\frac{d\tilde{W}}{2\pi i}e^{\frac{\tilde{Z}^{3}}{3}-u\tilde{Z}+\frac{\tilde{W}^{3}}{3}-v\tilde{W}}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{Z})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{Z})}\frac{(\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{\beta}-\tilde{W})}\frac{(\tilde{Z}-\tilde{W})}{(\tilde{Z}+\tilde{W})}\frac{1}{4\tilde{Z}\tilde{W}}.

(3.54)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\eta}_{2}\\
-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widetilde{\aleph}_{1}\quad\widetilde{\aleph}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\aleph}_{1}\\
\widetilde{\aleph}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}\quad\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\theta}_{2}\\
-\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widetilde{\aleph}_{1}\quad\widetilde{\aleph}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\aleph}_{1}\\
\widetilde{\aleph}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widetilde{\theta}_{2}\quad\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\eta}_{2}\\
-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widetilde{\psi}_{1}\quad\widetilde{\psi}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\psi}_{1}\\
\widetilde{\psi}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}\quad\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\theta}_{2}\\
-\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widetilde{\psi}_{1}\quad\widetilde{\psi}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\psi}_{1}\\
\widetilde{\psi}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widetilde{\theta}_{2}\quad\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\right|\right),

(3.55)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}:=

\displaystyle\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{2}+1\right)-\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\zeta}_{2}\\
-\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widetilde{\aleph}_{1}\quad\widetilde{\aleph}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\aleph}_{1}\\
\widetilde{\aleph}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widetilde{\zeta}_{2}\quad\widetilde{\zeta}_{1}\right|\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\NN}:=

\displaystyle-\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\right)\left(\widetilde{\NN}_{1}+\widetilde{\NN}_{2}\right)+\Pf\left(J-\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\eta}_{2}\\
-\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle\widetilde{\Theta}_{1}\quad\widetilde{\Theta}_{2}\right|-\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\widetilde{\Theta}_{1}\\
\widetilde{\Theta}_{2}\end{array}\right\rangle\left\langle-\widetilde{\eta}_{2}\quad\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\right|\right),

where Fredholm Pfaffian is taken over
\mathbb{L}^{2}((s,\infty)).

4.
Product stationary and Two-parameter stationary half-space log-gamma polymer models

Recall the partition function defined via the recurrence relation in (
1.1
). We introduce another equivalent definition of the partition function via weight collection along all the up-right paths.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
A_{0},A_{1},A_{2},\dots\in\mathbb{R}
be a sequence of parameters such that
A_{0}+A_{i}>0
for all
i\geq 1
and
A_{i}+A_{j}>0
for all
i>j\geq 1.
Define the octant
\mathcal{D}:=\{(i,j)\in\Z^{2}|1\leq j\leq i\}
. Let
(\omega_{i,j})_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{D}}
be a family of independent random variables such that
\omega_{i,j}\sim\text{Gamma}^{-1}(A_{i}+A_{j})
for
i>j
and
\omega_{i,i}\sim\text{Gamma}^{-1}(A_{0}+A_{i})
. The partition function of the half-space log-gamma polymer is defined as

(4.1)

Z(N,M)=\sum_{\pi:(1,1)\to(N,M)}\prod_{(i,j)\in\pi}\omega_{i,j},

where the sum is taken over up-right paths from
(1,1)
to
(N,M)
in
\mathcal{D}.
We call
\log Z(N,M)
the free energy of the model.

We invoke the symmetry property of the half-space log-gamma polymer model in the specific setting of our model. This symmetry arises from the fact that the law of the partition function is governed by the the Whittaker process. A more general statement and its proof can be found in
[
5
, Lemma 2.7]
.

Lemma 4.2
.

For any
N\in\Z_{\geq 2},
the law of
Z(N,N)
is invariant with respect to permutations of the parameters
A_{0},A_{1},\dots,A_{N}
.

Special realizations of parameters lead to product stationary model and two-parameter stationary model.
